#7452. The Traffic Pool Is Shrinking, And Which Channel Fills It Matters Less Than We Think via @sejournal, @DuaneForrester

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

AI referrals versus Google is a fair comparison of two streams feeding a smaller pool, and agents that complete the purchase are where the offset starts.

Late last month the industry spent a week debating a ratio, and seems to skip the number it sits on.

Three pieces of research landed within days of each other and carried an entire conversation. Marie Haynes looked at 12 months of GA4 data across 100 sites and more than 145 million sessions, and found that ChatGPT sent 0.62% of the sessions Google organic did, rising to 0.73% with every LLM included. The SEO Works benchmarked 95 sites and found organic search delivered 39 times the traffic of AI assistants. Kevin Indig ran 632 tests across 12 sites looking for AI visibility to show up later as branded search or direct traffic, and couldn’t find a reliable relationship. All three are careful pieces of work, generous with their data and candid about their limits, and I’d trust the numbers in each.

The conclusion the wider industry drew from them traveled faster than the work itself: AI sends almost nothing, so GEO is a distraction, so get back to Google. That reading answers a reasonable question: Which channel sends more visitors today, and the answer is plainly Google. What it leaves out is the size of the pool both channels draw from, and that pool is getting smaller.

Kevin, as it happens, published the clearest evidence of that a few days later. In his Similarweb analysis of 10,000 domains in each of the U.S. and France, organic search visits fell 13.2% in the U.S., and 24.4% in France between August 2024 and August 2026, and publishers lost roughly 41.5% of their organic traffic in the U.S. over the same two years. Paid search visits grew in the same data, and Alphabet’s third-quarter filing shows paid clicks still up 8% year over year, so the open web is losing visits from search while Google’s own paid inventory keeps growing, and no ratio between two referral channels will show you that. Kevin notes that paid growth replaced only about a third of the organic visits lost in the U.S., and that AI referrals, the fastest-growing channel in his sample, still account for about one visit in 200. Nothing on the search side of that picture refills the pool. His follow-up last week points to where some businesses are rebuilding it instead: email grew about 16% in his US sample, the steadiest gain of any channel, because it reaches an audience a business already owns.

Comparing AI referrals to Google organic tells you which of two inbound streams is smaller. It cannot tell you whether the total reaching your business is still enough to sustain it, and that second question is the one that decides budgets, headcount, and whether a traffic-dependent model survives the next three years.

The visits are not moving to a new channel so much as leaving the click altogether. A preprint from Bocconi University researchers Shi, Zhu and Gu, built on Comscore desktop clickstream data from more than 45,000 U.S. households, found that ChatGPT produced an outbound click in 5.2% of conversation sessions, compared with 31.1% of Google queries, and that wider access to ChatGPT Search reduced traditional search use by 9.4%. It’s a desktop-only preprint, but the direction matches everything else in hand: activity is moving to interfaces that answer in place and send fewer people anywhere.

Google’s own results page shows the same pull. Similarweb panel data for U.S. searches in early 2026 put the clicks reaching the open web at roughly 232 for every 1,000 searches. Last week I looked at Alphabet’s third quarter, where Google reported query growth at a five-year high, by a count it hasn’t defined, alongside its slowest search revenue growth of the year. More searching and fewer visits can both be true at once, and that pairing is the shrinking pool seen from Google’s side. Google’s head of Search said last year that total organic click volume to websites was relatively stable, but that claim came without numbers, and the Q3 call offered no figure on clicks sent to websites at all.

The decline is uneven, and that is the practical point. Kevin’s data shows the typical small site roughly flat while large sites and publishers absorbed most of the loss. The number to watch is your own pool: total inbound from every source, month over month, set against impressions for your category queries, so you can see whether demand is still there while the visits fall away. Nothing about tracking the pool needs a new vendor, though, and you can chart it this afternoon from your own analytics..

I argued recently that being indexed now earns a place in an answer rather than a visit. The shrinking pool is what that shift looks like in your reporting. Past a certain point, no mix of sources refills it, and the useful question changes from where your visits come from to whether the visit was ever the thing you needed. For most businesses it wasn’t. The visit was a step on the way to a booking, a purchase, or a signed form, and if that step is disappearing, the work moves to whatever completes the transaction without it.

Marie’s piece points exactly here. Alongside her data, she encouraged readers to spend their time preparing for the agentic web, naming UCP, Universal Cart, WebMCP, and intelligent eyewear, and I agree with her. Google is pointed the same way: on its Q3 earnings call, Sundar Pichai said agents will operate as a unifying layer across Google’s products, booking reservations, filling in forms, scheduling appointments, and making purchases. Agents acting on a searcher’s behalf are the most credible offset to a shrinking pool, not because they send traffic, but because they finish the job the traffic used to exist for. That changes the unit you are working toward, from the visit to the completed action, meaning the booking, order, or request an agent finalizes on someone’s behalf.

The clearest evidence of how agents choose comes from a McGill study by Davood Wadi and Yu Ma. They randomized the order of 100 hotel listings across 5,000 agent sessions on four large language models and compared the results with human data from Expedia. The agents searched more deeply than people and never once declined to book. Position still affected which listings they opened, but weakly and in an unexpected shape: the middle of the page was inspected least, not the bottom. Rank carried through to the final booking for only two of the four models. And every model converged on the same listing, the one nothing else beat on review score at its price, which took 78.2% of all bookings. The authors’ own conclusion is that for agentic search, the attributes displayed on a results page matter more than placement within it.

The same researchers found a second effect worth knowing before you assume agents are neutral shoppers. In a companion study, agents told they worked for a booking platform chose sponsored listings at roughly nine times the rate of agents told they worked for the traveler, 25% against 2.8%, with every other attribute matched. Whose agent it is shapes what it buys, and on the same Q3 call Google’s chief business officer said he’s excited about the advertising opportunities in an agentic world. A strong offer remains the price of entry everywhere, but on agents a platform deploys, sponsorship can still outrank it, so it pays to know which agents your buyers are actually using.

An AI agent does not need to find your business first. It needs to find nothing that beats your business on the attributes it can read, because agents converge on the undominated offer regardless of where that offer sits in a list.

That is also why the plumbing alone won’t carry anyone. A checkout protocol makes it easier to buy from you once an agent has decided to, and does nothing to make it decide. OpenAI ran a version of this experiment in public: It launched Instant Checkout inside ChatGPT in September 2025 and pulled back from it in March 2026, moving purchases back to merchants’ own sites and apps. The infrastructure matters, and it comes second.

Here is where that infrastructure stands today. Google’s Universal Cart launched in the U.S. on Oct. 15, with checkout running on the Universal Commerce Protocol, and on the Q3 call Google said UCP now enables direct checkout for hundreds of thousands of brands. That count almost certainly includes merchants enrolled through commerce platforms such as Shopify, because a public scan in May found 26 sites publishing a UCP profile of their own across more than 3 million. Both numbers can be true, and the gap is telling: If you sell through a platform, you may already be eligible without building anything, and if you don’t, very few competitors have built it themselves either. WebMCP, which lets a page expose actions such as booking or checking availability as tools an agent can call directly, is still an origin trial in Chrome, and Marie herself notes it may be early for most businesses to invest heavily there. Meanwhile, Cloudflare’s new defaults block agent traffic on ad-supported pages for new domains and for Free-tier sites that never changed their settings, so plenty of businesses are turning agents away without knowing it.

Earlier this fall, I declined to publish a checklist for measuring what AI does to your numbers, because there was no ground truth to score it against. This list is a different kind of thing. Every item is checkable, since an agent can either read your offer, trust it, and complete an action with you, or it can’t. The order runs offer first and plumbing last, with an honest maturity label on each.

None of this brings back the old volume, and it isn’t meant to. The businesses that come through a shrinking traffic pool will be the ones an agent can read, trust, and complete a transaction with, and that work starts with the offer rather than the protocol.

The debate over whether AI sends enough traffic has a fair answer: It doesn’t, and it probably won’t. The better use of a week of argument is to ask how much of your business still depends on the visit at all.

This post was originally published on Duane Forrester Decodes.

Duane Forrester is the Founder and CEO of UnboundAnswers.com, a consultancy helping businesses adapt to the realities of AI-powered search ...

#7449. The Nieman Lab News-About-News Quiz, November 13, 2026

All the latest headlines and insights from the world of digital journalism and publishing. Our daily newsletter is a must-read from newsrooms to executive suites.

We will never sell your address. Prefer a weekly email?

We have sent a confirmation link to . Click it and you are on the list. If it has not turned up in a minute or two, it may be in your spam folder.

From new majors to search traffic, newspapers to impeachment: Have you kept up with this week’s media news?

It’s been another busy week. Recap it with the second edition of the Nieman Lab News-About-News Quiz, your weekly incentive to keep up with the latest happenings in the world of digital journalism. All questions are based on the week’s Nieman Lab stories or articles elsewhere that we’ve linked to in What We’re Reading. The quiz will appear here through November as a trial run; if people seem to like it, we’ll keep it going in 2027. Good luck!

What journalism organization announced this week that it was canceling its annual conference and laying off most of its staff?

“Since we gathered in Chicago for ONA26, many of you have asked when we will be together again,” CEO Niketa Patel wrote. “Unfortunately, it will not be in 2027. ONA has decided to pause our in-person events and will not host a conference in 2027.”

For the first time ever, the press freedom group Reporters Without Borders (RSF) has officially called for the impeachment of a U.S. official. Which one?

“RSF does not take the question of impeachment lightly. In fact, our organization has never before called for the removal of a US official. But the severity and persistence of Carr’s misconduct as FCC chairman demand an extraordinary response. Congress has a constitutional responsibility to hold him accountable. Restoring the FCC’s independence must be central to that effort.”

Emerson College recently announced a new degree program aimed at helping students build “a career across a rapidly changing media landscape.” What’s the program’s name?

“Our new Content Creation major prepares students to be a part of the evolving creator economy, teaching them not only how to produce compelling digital content but also how to build a successful entrepreneurial career.”

What news organization — after a challenging few years — says it is now “within striking distance of break-even,” financially?

“I’ll be honest,” executive editor Matt Murphy said at a conference. “We had years of losses, and steepening losses. That’s the truth. We’re a private company and don’t share the figures, but it was hundreds and hundreds of millions of dollars. Now we’re within striking distance of break-even, and I expect next year will be our best year in seven years. We’ve done the right-sizing we needed, which was tough.”

The Echo-Pioneer — the local newspaper in the town of High Level, Alberta (population 4,000) — has an unusual business model. What is it?

Town officials signed a two-year “advertising and readership agreement” in 2025. It’s one of the many ways local Canadian news outlets are trying to stay afloat — most notably the federal Local Journalism Initiative.

Which online publisher’s CEO announced that Google search traffic had “dropped by more than 1 million referrals on average per week” in October?

As a result of the search decline, CEO Steve Huffman announced Reddit would be shifting to a primarily “logged-in experience.”

The Asia-Pacific outlet The Diplomat retracted an interview with a South Korean CEO last week. Why?

“The author presented themselves as a South Korean freelance journalist and claimed to have interviewed Newtype’s CEO. We now know that the journalist fabricated the interview and may not even be who they claimed to be.”

The parliament of an Asian nation is debating requiring tech companies like Google and Meta to pay news outlets when they direct users to their stories. Which nation?

The proposed law would also require compensating news publishers when AI models are trained on news content.

Which international news outlet, sensing a political opening, is expanding its reporting team in the United States?

“Market research indicated there was room to grow among politically liberal Jews in the United States. Gali Arnon, who became the chief executive of Haaretz Group last year, said she wanted the newspaper to become in other countries what it is in Israel: an ‘identity brand.’”

This photo shows an early editorial plan from one of the first important publications on the World Wide Web, a mononymic website that has recently been in the news. What site was it?

The photo is from Salon co-founder Laura Miller, laying out some early “issues” of the influential web “magazine” which closed last week. “People sometimes tell me about reading Salon avidly in their youth, how it felt like connecting to something exhilarating and cool and a bit crazy in the strange new expanses of the internet. All I can say is: That’s exactly how it felt to work there.”

The most important stories about journalism and the future of news, delivered to your inbox every afternoon.

ONA, OpenNews, and others see troubled times ahead, so it's goodbye to "rubber chicken dinners."

The new budget would make a local reporting program permanent and extend a 35% tax credit on newsroom salaries. The support makes “a massive, massive difference."

Help advance the Nieman Foundation's mission “to promote and elevate the standards of journalism” by making a donation.

#7448. Vertican: Senior Applied AI Engineer, Document Intelligence

For 40 years Vertican has buildt software that runs law firms. One year ago we created a new modern AI platform for document automation (processing/extraction/redacting) which our customers have been adopting faster than any product we've ever had. Our suite of AI-enabled products save customers thousands of hours each week by processing high volumes of documents with precision and speed.The AI team is growing and we'd like you to join.

We are hiring an engineer to own the document processing pipelines end to end: design, benchmarking, experimenting with new architectures and tools, threshold required for deploying a new pipeline, etc.

We believe it's important to build our own tooling to help us run, improve, and measure our applications. Therefore, the ideal candidate thinks outside the box on how to not just build the product/pipeline, but also the tooling needed around it.

You will be the first dedicated ML hire on the product taking over from the engineering manager who build the current solution and will work directly with the founding engineering team.

This is a senior, hands-on individual-contributor role, but we are small team and you'll be involved in design, planning, roadmapping, and architectural decisions.

Design the pipelines. Own the architecture of the extraction-to-posting pipelines: page classification, field extraction, candidate retrieval, claim matching, confidence gating and the hand-off to human review, etc. Decide where rules end and models begin, and what each stage must guarantee to the next, what the separation of concerns are, etc.

We have different pipelines for different document types and expect that at any given point in time we may have new ones emerge and may consolidate others.

Evaluate new systems before we adopt them. Run structured trials of candidate components and recommend what to keep. Examples include probabilistic record linkage (Splink), Azure AI Content Understanding and Document Intelligence, Azure OpenAI and Foundry-hosted models, and LLM agents with tools. Similarly, test new models with the existing pipeline as they are relased from the frontier labs.

Build the evaluation process. You'll own the process of evaluating false positives, false negatives, errors of ommission, etc, that we ultimately use to determine if we should upgrade the pipeline.

Ship and operate. Take components from trial to production in our python workers on the cloud, instrument them, watch cost and quota, and diagnose the cases reviewers send back.

Set direction. Work with leadereship on the planing for accuracy, cost improvement, and overall product improvements.

Small team, direct access to large datasets, weekly planning, daily standup, everyone remote in US timezones.

Vertican is an equal opportunity employer. We hire on merit and do not discriminate on the basis of race, color, religion, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, national origin, age, disability or veteran status.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

#7446. Microsoft Advertising Updates UI Following Advertiser Feedback via @sejournal, @brookeosmundson

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Microsoft Advertising announces UI improvements addressing advertiser feedback, including easier navigation, expanded reporting, sign-in troubleshooting, and account access management.

Microsoft Advertising is updating its platform interface following advertiser feedback about navigation, reporting, and account access issues.

In its November 9 product newsletter, Microsoft acknowledged several problems advertisers have faced, including trouble finding reports and resolving sign-in issues.

The updates include easier navigation, expanded campaign reporting, clearer account permissions, and better sign-in troubleshooting.

Microsoft says more improvements are planned as part of its ongoing interface updates.

Microsoft is making changes to how advertisers move through the platform and review campaign performance.

The updated navigation menu groups features by common tasks and makes more options available by default. New breadcrumbs show where users are within their account and campaign settings.

Advertisers can also use navigation search and customize menus to reach frequently used features faster.

Microsoft acknowledged that its previous reporting tools made it difficult to find, compare, and export certain data.

The bidding updates could help advertisers understand performance changes, especially when automated bidding targets have changed over time.

Microsoft is also responding to feedback about account access problems, including issues that have prevented some advertisers from signing in.

The newsletter specifically mentions blank pages, unexpected redirects, and repeated sign-in attempts.

To help resolve these issues, Microsoft is adding clearer error messages, browser troubleshooting tips, and more specific recovery steps.

Microsoft is also working to make sign-ins more persistent, although it hasn’t shared details about how login behavior will change.

Account permissions and user management are getting updates as well.

Advertisers will see clearer information about user roles, permissions, and who can grant access to an account.

Invitation pages will provide more details about required permissions, expiration dates, and next steps. Administrators will also have better visibility into pending and expired invitations.

These changes could be helpful for agencies and in-house teams managing access across several accounts, especially when adding new users.

Microsoft also acknowledged feedback that some account recommendations have been too generic.

It’s working to improve when recommendations appear and explain why a specific change is being suggested for an account.

Some of these updates address common frustrations with Microsoft Advertising, while others provide more detail for reviewing campaign performance.

The bidding reports are one of the more useful additions, especially for advertisers using Target CPA or Target ROAS.

Seeing past targets alongside actual performance and conversion-delay data could help explain changes that aren’t obvious in standard campaign reports.

The search term reporting improvements should also provide more insight into how AI Max campaigns match queries with landing pages.

On the account management side, clearer sign-in instructions and permission settings could reduce the time spent troubleshooting access issues.

Microsoft reiterated that some of these features continue to rollout, so it’s possible not every advertiser has seen the updates in their account UI.

For advertisers who have struggled with reporting or account access, these changes address some familiar problems. How well the new experience works will become clearer as more advertisers gain access to the updates.

Brooke serves as the Director of Growth Marketing at Smith Micro Software, with over 10 years of paid media experience. ...

#7441. Visits To The Top Million Websites Fell 12%, Data Shows via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Data shows visits to the world's top million websites declined from an earlier peak, visits going to sites outside the top 100 barely changed.

Visits to the world’s top million websites were lower in the third quarter of 2026 than at their early 2022 peak (a decline of 12.2%), based on the data from Similarweb shared by SparkToro. The proportion of visits that go to websites that are not among the top 100 stayed relatively consistent over this time. That means while the total number of visits was down, that proportion still added up to less traffic for non-top-100 sites.

Rand Fishkin, SparkToro’s co-founder and CEO, wrote that the project began with his theory that the biggest sites, largely Big Tech properties, were taking more of the web’s traffic.

The post also reports a 29.2% drop in visits per internet user. This calculation uses user estimates that were revised partway through the period.

Fishkin asked Similarweb for total visits to the top million sites from its worldwide desktop and mobile web panel, along with a breakdown by site rank.

In the post’s figures, total visits peaked in Q1 2022 at 2.243 trillion. At the low point in Q1 2026, the total was 1.914 trillion, down 14.7% from the peak. The latest quarter, Q3 2026, came in at 1.970 trillion, 12.2% below the peak.

The totals on the post’s chart stay roughly level through 2023, then dip in early 2024 and fall again in the first half of 2025.

Similarweb categorized the top million websites from the number 1-10 to the 100,001st to 1 millionth website by five levels of ranking.

The share chart shows roughly a combined 33% and 36% of visits for the #1-10 (top) and #11-100 (next 90) sites during Q4 2021 and Q3 2026 respectively. The percentages for each band below the top 100 were a little lower at the end of the period compared to the beginning.

Fishkin described growth in the share for the top 100 as small, writing that “stagnation is a more accurate description of the trend.”

SparkToro’s per-user number is found by dividing Similarweb’s total visits by DataReportal’s estimated quarterly internet users globally. The table included in the blog post reports 4.95 billion internet users in Q4 2021 and 6.15 billion for Q3 2026. This represents an increase of around 24% during that time. The blog post reports the decline in visits per user at 29.2%.

The user series jumped from 5.65 billion to 6.04 billion in one quarter (Q3) of 2025. According to DataReportal in their October 2025 report, this jump in the number of users online was “largely attributable to significant revisions” in the rate of internet use that were officially reported by India and China. They also warned the reader to avoid interpreting this change as if hundreds of millions of new people came online in 90 days.

The SparkToro post did not include information regarding the change in reporting. Their estimate of 24% user growth compared data prior to and subsequent to the reporting change.

Similarweb’s panel tracks browser visits on desktop and mobile, but not app use. Fishkin wrote that consolidation by Big Tech is likely much more common in apps.

The data is also worldwide, and he wrote that it’s plausible traffic to U.S., U.K. and EU sites declined while regions with growing internet access, such as Asia and Africa, grew. The post doesn’t break out regions or state the panel’s size.

He did not provide reasons for the decline in visits. Fishkin’s list of possible factors includes app-only use, reliance on search engine results, AI tool utilization, social media platforms and the user behavior changes after the COVID-19 pandemic.

After initial publication SparkToro updated their article. On Nov. 10, Fishkin said on X that the prior version of his post “had an incorrect graph/data.” That version’s headline put the decline at 24%, which the URL still shows.

SparkToro sells audience research software, and Fishkin co-authored the book “Zero Click Marketing.”

SparkToro has provided an international benchmark for when a multi-year traffic decline shows up in your reports. The percentage of visits based upon the ranking of the website held close to steady in Similarweb’s browser-based metrics, although the actual number of visits decreased. The headline statistic in the report without reliance on changes in user estimates is the 12.2 percent decline in overall visits.

This report measures the number of browser visits to the top million websites regardless of where they came from, whereas the zero-click data I reported on back in June measured clicks per U.S. Google search.

Until a regional breakdown is available, SparkToro’s figures are best viewed as global context for traffic conversations, rather than as a benchmark for any single market. Fishkin also wrote that he plans to compare the findings with other sources and revisit the numbers every year or two.

#7437. Does The Affiliate Model Survive Checkout Inside AI Mode And Gemini? – Ask An SEO via @sejournal, @rollerblader

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Affiliate links still help protect your commissions as AI checkout grows, while stronger tracking and better program choices help preserve referral credit.

This week’s Ask an SEO is about affiliate marketing, which is actually one of my favorite topics to cover.

“With Google’s UCP and checkout happening inside Gemini and AI Mode, the shopper never clicks my link. Does the affiliate model survive this, and what should I be doing now?”

Great question, and yes, affiliate links still matter, so make sure you keep using them. It is similar to Shopify having the Google Direct Checkout UCP. That doesn’t mean you don’t need to keep your Shopify store, but you also want to make sure you are protected with multiple ways to monetize and that you work with brands that focus on more than just links.

This is why there is a question. If the person doesn’t make it to the link, do links matter? Yes, and this goes back to the history of links.

I was on a call once where we decided that if there was an affiliate parameter and if it was on the browser, the website would automatically prompt the person to log in. This was high-tech back then.

The message displayed was a benefit to them, to let them know that if they logged in, they could add to their cart and check out later when they have more time vs. needing to do everything right now. When they logged in, we could sync up the sale with the original click, and the affiliate would get credit. This led to fingerprinting and many more advanced techniques the networks (like Buy.at at the time) began working on.

There are also affiliate companies that show ads via streaming TV shows and track the IPs connected to the same connection to track affiliate sales back. They count the IPs against consumer data sent back to the network and get credit for the sale.

The reason I mention this is because, clicks don’t always matter, but they do add levels of tracking security for you. There is IP hashing, companies like TV Scientific and AudioHook are using alternative tracking methods, and vanity codes. Just make sure your vanity code doesn’t get leaked to coupon websites, browser extensions, and that AI doesn’t start showing them to traffic you didn’t refer.

See also: When Attribution Looks More Precise Than The Data Behind It

Being afraid of Google taking the order and cutting your link out is a valid concern. But think about TikTok Shop, or retailer apps inside ChatGPT like Walmart’s, which keep the purchase on the platform. These cut your link out completely. The person may read your content then go to TikTok for a review and purchase there.

You have big brands that allow third parties to sell on their website (i.e., marketplaces), and with some you don’t get commissions for those sales as they’re third party vendors. Your links matter and clicks tracked, but the conversion is not commissionable. In the case of systems like TikTok, use links on your website and upload content to their platform to make money with your vendors that are synced there. You control the situation, don’t stress over a single link on the website.

For programs that allow third-party sales and don’t pay, select other programs. That is a choice you are knowingly making and you are knowingly sending your traffic there.

See also: Getting Your Product Into ChatGPT Isn’t The Hard Part, Getting It Through Checkout Is

Another reason to not stop using affiliate links is that consumers do not trust AI. Keynote speakers at college graduations keep getting booed when they talk about it; younger generations are avoiding brands that use it for creating images; people are starting to engage less with AI ads on social media and convert less when the content and images are clearly AI on a website (blog posts, product pages, etc.).

When AI is used, there’s no way to know if the store and ad are legit, or if they’re a third-party counterfeit produced overseas. Reviews are also being gamed. Consumers may not trust the AI Overview as much if quality doesn’t improve, and if they don’t like AI, they will instead scroll down to your content, where they can be exposed to your link.

There is another layer to affiliate links mattering as well.

AI Overviews cannot demonstrate a product or service. Yes, they can play a video, but Google search has done this for years. People, especially those who do not trust AI, will keep looking for human-created content before making the purchase. This is especially true for larger-ticket items like bikes, software, or computer systems, and demonstrations of video games before buying them. Think of parents who want to make sure it is age-appropriate, or someone who wants to see what the graphics and games are like from an actual gamer.

This makes your affiliate links directly relevant, and in your advertising disclosure you can let them know that by clicking your link they are helping to support your business and not AI. This will resonate strongly with the anti-AI generations and shoppers. It is also required by the FTC and some international groups that you use advertising disclosures, so you might as well.

See also: Only 28% Of Americans Trust AI Search – And That Gap Is Your SEO Opening

There is affordable technology from third parties like ThoughtMetrics (I have zero relationship with them, just a happy user) that shows all touchpoints in a customer journey (and repeat purchases for LTV). If you didn’t use your affiliate link, you won’t be in the running for credit or a commission. In another program, we reconcile at the end of the month and manually add anything that didn’t track. If that click didn’t happen, you would not be making as much money.

Link clicks are always going to be an issue, but they are also still working and a first line of defense in your ability to get credit for your referrals. So yes, affiliate links and clicks still matter; there are just additional layers of protection available with more advanced tracking techniques.

Featured Image: Paulo Bobita/Search Engine Journal

#7436. Meta publishes explainer on Your Algorithm controls

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The feature enables users to opt in or out of certain topics and customize their feeds based on personal preferences.

Meta has shared some new insights into how its Your Algorithm control option on Facebook works, which enables users to provide direct input into what content they want to see more or less of in the app.

The tool enables users to add or remove specific topics from an automatically generated list of interests, which Facebook's system has built based on each user's in-app actions. In addition to removing and adding topics, users can also now tell the algorithm specifically what they want to see more of, via a recently added chatbot prompt.

Conceptually, this gives users more ways to refine their Facebook experience.

On the Meta engineering blog, the company has shared notes on how the process works, and explained more about how exactly updating user algorithm preferences can impact in-app experiences.

In addition to these topic-related notes, Meta said the system also used demotion rules to enact more general parameters that don’t relate to a specific topic.

“Quality-related requests, such as reducing AI-generated or sexually suggestive content, map to dedicated demotion signals rather than topic matching,” Meta said.

These demotion rules work differently than topic ranking, with the system using these as indicators to reduce these elements.

“During ranking, the same embedding-based comparison used in the relevance check removes candidates too close to an interest in Not Interested In, and demotion rules push down near matches,” Meta said.

In other words, this feature can reduce the presence of artificial intelligence-generated content in a user’s Facebook feed, though it won’t get rid of that entirely, and ranking will still be influenced by future engagement.

When a user requests less content from a specific creator, however, “that creator’s content is filtered out directly,” Meta said.

These notes provide more insight into how Meta plans to facilitate algorithm customization and give users more control over their in-app experiences.

Meta’s hope is that through tools like this, users will feel more empowered in the app. In turn, that may quell ongoing calls for the platform to return to a reverse chronological feed and remove ranking elements entirely.

Given that algorithmic amplification has continued to drive up Facebook and Instagram usage, Meta is very keen to keep that in place.

Though the company may not have a choice. Last month, the Australian government began preliminary investigations into potential new laws that would force social media platforms to provide users with an option to switch off algorithmic recommendations and set non-algorithmic feeds as the default.

A growing body of research suggests that algorithmic amplification can have harmful impacts. If this new push in Australia goes ahead and it ends up showing that the negative impacts of algorithms are reduced, that could lead to more regions exploring similar rules.

As noted, Meta is very keen to avoid this outcome.

Last year on a Q2 earnings call with analysts, Meta CEO Mark Zukerberga said AI recommendations had led to a 5% increase in time spent on Facebook, and a 6% boost on Instagram for the quarter.

On its Q2 earnings call a year later, Zuckerberg said that global time spent on Instagram had grown in the quarter by double digits year over year, and Facebook video time spent had increased by 9% globally, "largely driven by improvements to our feed and Reels recommendations."

It makes sense, then, that Meta is keen to avoid any external push to switch off its algorithmic ranking.

As such, Meta's hoping that the introduction of this control option will ease user concerns. Combine this with the fact that, based on past history, users rarely update their settings, and the company's likely hoping that it can benefit from the perception of control afforded by the tool, while also seeing a minor impact on its business.

#7432. X experiments with expanded AI transparency labels

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company is testing an automated way to detect and tag posts that may have been generated using artificial intelligence.

X is developing new AI transparency labels for posts, which will not only indicate whether AI was used to generate an update, but also how it was applied in each instance.

Michael O’Herlihy, X’s head of brand safety, shared some examples of these new labels during a presentation at the Brand & AI Safety Summit in New York on Tuesday.

As shown on these example screens, posted by Adweek senior tech reporter Kendra Barnett on X, X’s new AI content labels will include more detailed information on why a post was flagged for AI content. This will better explain the rationale X’s automated enforcement system is using, while also giving creators more opportunities to refute these labels by offering them a more detailed understanding of any potential concerns.

The hope is that these expanded notes will help X refine its AI detection process by prompting more creators to provide feedback on the specifics. This information can then be used to train the system.

At the same time, the tags will add more transparency for users, who may be less likely to re-share a post that’s been flagged for AI content.

O’Herlihy added that X increased its reliance on automated tagging of AI violations because it’s much faster. The automation should also ensure that more harmful content is refuted in-stream.

It could be a good way to enhance transparency in the AI era. However, false positives could also be problematic for creators, who could get annoyed if their content is unfairly tagged.

#7430. Google Makes Gemini 4 Hydrogen The Default Model In AI Mode via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Google made Gemini 4 Hydrogen the default model in AI Mode for all users and uses it for the hardest AI Overview questions, two days into a core update.

Google made Gemini 4 Hydrogen the default model in AI Mode for all users worldwide, starting today. Sites that track how often AI Mode cites them now have a new model to account for.

In an X thread announcing the change, Robby Stein, VP of Product for Google Search, said Hydrogen is also “being used for your hardest questions in AI Overviews.” Google’s Search post says the model is available globally in Search for all users.

The switch came two days into the November core update, which was still rolling out as of 2:05 p.m. ET today.

The Search post says Hydrogen is able to handle multi-part inquiries, inquiries that contain typos, highly specific inquiries, and follow commands more thoroughly than previous versions of Search.

The post from Search says that Hydrogen also allows for better query fan-out. Query fan-out refers to how Search simultaneously executes multiple queries across each of the subtopics of an inquiry. According to the post, the result is what Google calls “connecting you to a broader range of helpful links than before,” and this includes websites which users may have never stumbled upon themselves. Stein’s thread says that the fan-out improvements result in showing more links to the web. Neither the post nor Stein’s thread provide counts for links or sites.

The post references more visual aids in answers through use of images, structured responses, comparison tables and additional customized tools available to users; additionally, the answers provided will be more nuanced in all languages supported by AI Mode.

The last default change I covered was at I/O back in May when Gemini 3.5 Flash became the global default for AI Mode for everyone. Neither the Search post nor Stein’s post said what Hydrogen replaced.

Stein’s thread has Hydrogen handling the hardest AI Overview questions but doesn’t say which questions count as hardest or what answers the rest. The Search post doesn’t mention AI Overviews.

Google made Gemini 3 the default model for AI Overviews globally in January. At the time, I noted that Google had described plans to send complex questions to Gemini 3 and simpler ones to faster models, and hadn’t said whether that would apply to AI Overviews.

Citation data you pull for AI Mode or AI Overviews this month now spans a model change on Nov. 12. The change applies to AI Mode’s default answers and to the hardest AI Overview questions.

Nov. 12 falls inside the core update window, which began Nov. 10. Logging both dates lets you compare your tracking data before and after each one, rather than reading this month as a single change.

Google’s claim that there are more links doesn’t include figures. You can check how it applies to your site by looking at the citation and referral numbers in your own reports.

Although the Search Status Dashboard shows that the November core update is rolling out, its completion date will be logged as the second date, after Nov. 12.

You should watch for an announcement from Google on which AI Overview questions are classified as hardest so you can see how much of your AI Overview tracking will reflect the change.

#7427. Elon Musk’s Grok AI faces legal challenges over nudification

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Multiple plaintiffs allege that the chatbot is directly responsible for creating naked images based on photos taken of them as children, per Bloomberg.

Elon Musk’s xAI is facing questions regarding who is ultimately responsible for the generation of harmful content via generative artificial intelligence tools. At the heart of these issues is whether the fault lies with the person who tells the system to create this material, or the platform that facilitates this functionality.

These problems lie at the core of multiple legal cases involving xAI’s Grok chatbot, with plaintiffs in three U.S. states seeking legal recourse against xAI over the creation of CSAM material.

As reported by Bloomberg, xAI has come under investigation in three high-profile cases.

The company has denied responsibility in these cases, and said these depictions were generated by users, not by xAI itself.

Musk, xAI’s owner, has also made similar public statements.

In January, amid reports that Grok was being used to generate thousands of nudified images every day, Musk posted: “Anyone using Grok to make illegal content will suffer the same consequences as if they upload illegal content.”

However, shortly after making that announcement, and facing a potential ban of X apps from app stores, xAI changed its policies and usage restrictions on Grok to ensure that it could no longer be used to generate nudified images at scale.

The change in approach showed that xAI had the capacity to restrict this functionality whenever it wanted. However, the company instead initially refused to restrict Grok usage on free speech grounds, and settled on user autonomy as its legal defense.

Indeed, xAI has even challenged new laws designed to outlaw the generation of non-consensual naked images.

As reported by Reuters last month, xAI succeeded in blocking the implementation of Minnesota’s proposed ban on AI-generated naked images by arguing that the law was unconstitutional and infringed on free speech rights. The company was the only provider actively opposing this law.

At the same time, xAI has repeatedly touted Grok’s systematic capacity to generate what is called “spicy” content or offensive material in its “unhinged mode.” The company has also launched companion chatbots that can engage in NSFW chats.

As such, this does seem like more of a feature than a bug, yet xAI is hoping it can hang onto the fact that it’s not liable for usage that goes beyond certain stated parameters.

Establishing legal responsibility could set a new precedent in AI image generation, and it will be interesting to see whether any of these cases results in xAI being held responsible.

#7424. Google Ads Rolls Out New Invalid Traffic Overview Card via @sejournal, @brookeosmundson

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Google Ads introduces an Invalid Traffic Overview Card showing estimated savings, post-billing credits, and campaign-level reports. Here's what advertisers need to know.

Google Ads is rolling out a new Invalid Traffic Overview Card that shows advertisers how much invalid activity its systems filter before billing, along with credits issued after billing.

Until now, advertisers had to look across campaign reports and monthly billing statements to find that information. The new card brings those figures into one view, with additional reporting available by day and campaign.

Ginny Marvin, Google Ads Liaison, announced the rollout on LinkedIn and shared more details in the latest Ads Decoded newsletter.

The global rollout began this week, and the feature is also available in Search Ads 360 for Google Ads accounts.

Advertisers can find the new card on the Google Ads Overview page, under the Campaigns tab.

Google has long used automated systems and manual reviews to filter invalid ad interactions, including bot activity, accidental clicks, and attempts to artificially inflate traffic.

Most of that activity is caught before billing, so advertisers aren’t charged for those interactions.

The new card includes three reporting areas:

Clicking “View details” opens a daily trend chart and campaign table. Advertisers can also access the monthly invoices where credits were applied.

The estimated savings figure is the main addition to this information. Advertisers could already review invalid click metrics, but those reports didn’t show the estimated dollar amount Google had prevented from being charged.

According to Marvin, Google’s systems catch the majority of invalid traffic before it reaches an advertiser’s invoice.

Google Ads locks standard campaign metrics at month-end to match billing records. If Google detects more invalid traffic after that, any credits issued won’t change the Cost or CPA figures in the main Campaigns table.

Advertisers who want to see those adjustments reflected in their performance metrics can use the Invalid Activity Credit: Search & PMax report.

Available in Report editor, it includes 10 columns, such as Adjusted cost and Adjusted cost / conv. Credits are attributed to the dates the ads originally ran, even when Google issued them later.

This gives advertisers a way to review Search and Performance Max costs with post-billing invalid activity credits factored in, without relying solely on the standard Campaigns table.

The campaign-level breakdowns should make it easier to see where Google is filtering invalid activity, although the estimated savings figures will need some context.

A campaign with significantly more spend could show higher estimated savings simply because it’s generating more traffic. Comparing those dollar amounts alone won’t necessarily tell advertisers which campaigns have greater traffic quality concerns.

There’s also a limit to what the numbers can tell us. The card reflects activity Google’s systems have identified as invalid, but it doesn’t establish how much fraudulent traffic may have gone undetected.

For lead generation accounts, invalid traffic reporting won’t necessarily explain why fake or low-quality leads are continuing to come in.

Marvin addressed this in the Ads Decoded newsletter: Google’s systems can filter invalid ad interactions, but bad leads can still come from legitimate clicks or submissions that never originated from an ad.

An account could have relatively little invalid traffic reported in Google Ads and still struggle with lead quality. Advertisers dealing with those issues will need to compare the reporting against their CRM data rather than assuming Google’s invalid traffic figures tell the whole story.

Featured image: Primakov (screenshot from Google Ads Decoded Newsletter) / Shutterstock

#7422. LinkedIn tests frequent viewer feature

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Premium users already see who views their profile, but a new report from Wired said the platform may also soon show how often someone stops by.

LinkedIn users may soon be able to derive more value from its “Who viewed your profile” list, with the platform working on a new feature that would not only show who visited their profile, but also how often each specific user revisited their page within a given period.

That would add more context to LinkedIn’s profile viewer data, although for most users, this is fairly useless information. However, some marketers have highlighted this as a lead-generation opportunity, providing a list of potential warm contacts, based on the fact that they’ve checked out a user’s profile.

A frequency indicator on the same display would enhance that capacity, by showing people who have returned to a user’s profile in the app again and again, which could be an indicator of even stronger interest.

So long as that interest is in a professional capacity.

A visit count for each profile listed could also show users who could be interested in them for other reasons, which could make this a somewhat unsettling display.

LinkedIn users can lock down their profile to connections only, so there would be ways to address this concern, and of course users are under no obligation to contact people who’ve visited their profile.

Wired said that LinkedIn confirmed the feature is currently in testing, but it had no further info to share at this stage.

#7420. Thought leadership remains highly valuable for B2B buyers

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

A new survey report from Edelman and LinkedIn found that brands value professional insight and experience from other professionals.

Edelman has published a new report which examines the impact of thought leadership content on LinkedIn, and how professionals are utilizing thought leader content within their decision-making process.

Based on insights from “more than 1,100 management-level professionals,” Edelman’s 2026 B2B Thought Leadership Impact Report provides notes on the shifting B2B landscape. In particular, it looks at the role professional expertise plays in the artificial intelligence era.

According to the report, that role is still significant.

Edelman’s data showed that 82% of buyers believe that thought leadership is becoming more valuable, with original research, insight and experience now being more of a differentiator.

The data also showed that 89% of buyers view high-quality thought leadership content “makes a company feel like a long-term partner worth choosing.”

What’s more, a brand that doesn’t share thought leadership content can risk being eliminated from consideration for some potential partners.

And while AI tools are now playing a bigger role in the discovery process, the data also showed that most buyers still validate AI recommendations by referring to a brand’s content.

Also worth noting: Buyers are more likely to share thought leadership content in private channels.

There are also some interesting notes on the value of external influencers when it comes to endorsing a business, and the importance of brands continuing to produce thought leadership content to reinforce existing partnerships.

In summary, while product research is changing, and more business buyers are using AI tools to find relevant products, original brand content still plays a key role in winning deals and assuring buyers that they’re making the right decision.

The key differentiation, then, is expertise and unique insight that nobody else can provide. Even in the age of AI, where digital systems can summarize information,  AI can’t provide real, hands-on experience and insights. It also can’t summarize information that doesn’t already exist.

Hence the need for more thought leadership-type content, and for brands to differentiate themselves through expanded sharing of their professional expertise.

#7417. What Reddit Reviews Reveals About Your Products via @sejournal, @brentcsutoras

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Spot recurring product complaints and competitor comparisons with Reddit Reviews, then check source comments for context before making marketing decisions.

If Reddit’s taught us anything over the last five years, it’s that people really do care about validating the assumptions that they’re making along their journey. They really want to hear from real users who have actually used something or tried something, what their experience was, even if they don’t know who those users are.

People want to see the questions, complaints, and comparisons that come up when they are deciding what to buy. Reddit Reviews brings those conversations together on one page, giving people another way to find those experiences and click through to the original discussions.

On Nov. 10, Reddit announced the Reviews test with summaries of product conversations, themes about what people like and dislike, commonly compared products, and comments that lead back to their original discussions. Reddit says the initial rollout will reach U.S. users in English on web, iOS, and Android. It covers about 10,000 products across electronics, health and beauty, home and garden, and fashion and apparel. It plans to add products and categories and expand beyond the U.S. during 2027.

That can make research easier to inspect. A shopper can move from a summary to the comment, see the community where it appeared, and follow the details that matter to them. Reddit says some pages will also include images, prices, and purchase links supplied through brand and retailer catalogs. Those details are worth checking, especially when a page’s price or product information does not match the retailer’s current listing.

See also: Why Your Product Feed Is An SEO Asset (And Who Should Own It)

Reddit Reviews can help surface patterns, but there are limitations explained in the page FAQs. A verified purchase is not required; Reddit does not confirm that someone bought or used a product, and AI can miss context. The sentiment display gives a quick view of included conversations, while the detailed comments show what went into it. A review cannot represent every customer or provide an exact percentage.

Before drawing a conclusion from a review about your brand, I would ask: Is the same complaint coming up in different threads? Is the summary missing a condition that changes the recommendation? Are people comparing your product with a competitor for a reason your team has not been tracking? That context is where the useful research is.

A moderator’s concern was raised in the launch discussion about figuring out whether product recommendations come from people with firsthand experience. One commenter said verifying whether book recommendations came from people who had read the books was “already a gigantic challenge.” The moderator’s comment does not measure how Reddit Reviews performs, but it does point to a real trust question for any system summarizing community advice.

See also: The Review Gap: Finding Client Opportunities In Competitor Feedback

I would treat these review pages as another place for brands to listen. Check whether a review exists for the products and categories you care about, compare its summary with the source comments, note the products people consider alongside yours, and check whether the merchant information is current. Then compare recurring questions and objections with what support, product, and research teams already hear.

That can tell you where expectations and experience diverge. It cannot tell you what all customers think, how many people bought, or whether Reviews changed a purchase. Reddit says the initial pages draw from eligible conversations and that businesses cannot pay for product inclusion.

There is no conversion result to claim yet. So, watch how review pages appear in Reddit search, and check the referral and conversion data available on your own site before drawing conclusions about sales.

This is also a reason to make the community conversations themselves better. Give people room to ask real questions, share tradeoffs, and explain what did or did not work. If your team participates, be clear about who you are and help where you are welcome. My earlier piece on Reddit and the purchase journey makes the same point: People use Reddit to validate a decision, and the experience behind the recommendation is what gives that conversation value.

This is why we work with all of our clients to make sure that we’re building a community for them where their users have a place to come and share and talk about their products and their experiences.

When you spot a recurring product problem or a comparison buyers keep making, take it back to the team that can do something with it. That is the kind of information I think marketers can actually use.

Brent Csutoras is the founder of OGS Media, co-founder of ZipTie.ai, and Managing Partner at Search Engine Journal. He helps ...

Breaking news and insights to help you lead search strategies.

#7415. Pinterest adds holiday shopping tools

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform updated its chatbot with trending gift guides and introduced product comparison tools and wish lists.

Pinterest has added some new shopping features for the holidays, including trending gift guides within its Pinterest Assistant chatbot, product comparison tools and wishlists.

First, Pinterest added festive season activations to its Pinterest Assistant chatbot, including full-screen overviews of seasonal concepts based on trends.

As shown in this example screen, the new holiday experience in Pinterest Assistant offers dedicated trending content overviews for gift inspiration, seasonal decorating ideas and more.

Each highlighted product connects to a shoppable Pin, which could provide additional guidance for some of the most common app use cases during the season.

Pinterest also added a compare feature, which provides Pinterest Assistant users with a range of products to consider based on their queries.

The process gives users a carousel-like display of options to choose from, with reviews as well as information on price and availability.

Pinterest said that Compare is available through Pinterest Assistant and boards on mobile, and through the Close-up button for desktop users in the U.S.

Pinterest also added Wishlists, where shoppers can list their ideal product Pins, and get notified about price drops or restocks.

As per Pinterest: “People can create a wishlist to share with family or use it to keep track of gifts for everyone on their list.”

Pinterest said that Wishlists are currently available in a limited iOS test across the U.S. and Canada.

The platform is also rolling out Deal Boards, which will highlight personalized lists of trending deals based on past engagement history. Pinterest will also include a range of curated deals from creators throughout the season.

Finally, Pinterest shared a list of some of the top gift trends, based on increased in-app search activity, that it’s seen thus far this holiday shopping season.

Pinterest has repeatedly highlighted its increasing value as a product discovery and shopping channel. As such, the holiday season is a key time for the app to showcase that value.

These new features are designed to help more people get seasonal inspiration in the app, and helpful alerts on price drops and stock updates could also provide value.

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company said that its connected TV ads are 2.6 times more likely to reach B2B audiences than other CTV options.

LinkedIn announced an expansion of its connected TV ad offering, with U.K.-based businesses now also able to launch CTV campaigns.

LinkedIn’s CTV ads let advertisers activate video campaigns that will be displayed on their audience’s home TV sets via a range of display partners.

For the U.K. market, LinkedIn is partnering with LG, Paramount+, Titan OS and others.

LinkedIn advertisers will be able to launch CTV campaigns via LinkedIn Campaign Manager, with LinkedIn’s system able to present ads to “eligible viewers watching ad-supported streaming TV across participating publishers,” per the company.

LinkedIn launched CTV ads with U.S. advertisers in April 2024 as another means to tap into rising video consumption trends and expand campaign reach beyond its app.

LinkedIn also shared research highlighting the value of CTV ads in reaching relevant audiences.

According to the company, 81% of global B2B CMOs have indicated that video is helping to increase the sales cycle and drive impact. In addition, people who saw a brand’s CTV ad first cost that brand 30% less to convert into sales leads.

LinkedIn also noted that its CTV ads are 2.6 times more effective at building brand awareness among B2B audiences than other CTV options.

The expansion of LinkedIn’s CTV offering will facilitate more opportunities, helping to extend marketing pushes beyond the app.

YouTube's looking to tap into increased creator interest around the launch of the latest Grand Theft Auto game, with the platform set to host a continuous, 24-hour launch day livestream for GTA VI that will feature a range of top gaming creators.

The global-scale gaming stream will be promoted in the app and users can tap on a custom GTA VI-themed YouTube logo on the homepage to view the stream.

The stream will offer a showcase of gaming creators as well as a look at the new game. It will also provide fans with a means to link into the broader community excitement surrounding the launch.

In addition to the streaming event, YouTube is launching limited-edition, game-themed digital gifts, “including iconic animations like Wanted Stars that pop up directly on screen during streams,” the company said.

It’s also added a “GTA: Visit Vice City” Shorts Effect, which will enable Shorts creators to depict themselves within the game world. Users will also be able to access the GTA VI soundtrack on the YouTube Music homepage throughout launch week.

YouTube also published a new Culture and Trends report exploring the rise of GTA-related content across the app.

Gaming culture has played a major role in the rise of YouTube, with gaming and gaming-related creators always featured heavily among its top channels.

The GTA series, in particular, has prompted a heap of YouTube content, with the platform claiming that videos related to the series have generated more than 900 billion views in the app.

It makes sense, then, for YouTube to give this long-awaited launch a specific focus and highlight broader community engagement in the app.

#7406. Senior Product Builder - Hub at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Camunda Hub is where enterprises run Camunda at scale. From Hub they manage their clusters and tenants, decide who can do what, and roll changes out across the whole organization. We’re looking for a Senior Product Builder to own the backend and architecture that let Hub manage a whole fleet of clusters. Your first focus will be the mechanism that sends access, secret, tenant and licence changes out to every connected cluster and brings deployments, health and audit records back. From there, you’ll shape Hub’s foundations: its event model, the service boundaries and data model other teams build on, and how Hub behaves when things fail or get upgraded. The platform is your product. Your users are platform administrators, Centres of Excellence and the builders across Camunda who depend on what you create. You’ll join a new two-person pod in our remote-first team. You’ll have real ownership as the person who makes the call, and you’ll build AI-first, from written specs through to production. Our customers run mission-critical processes on Camunda, so your work will matter to them every day.

Take over Hub's fleet propagation mechanism from the team building its first version, and make it work for access, secrets, tenants, licences and custom roles

Write short design documents for foundational changes, get them reviewed by the teams that depend on them, and turn them into tested, shipped code

Design how Hub behaves when things go wrong: offline clusters, partial failures, Hub outages, and clusters being adopted or detached

Build the path from clusters back to Hub, starting with deployment inventory, health, audit records and licence status

Design Hub's first event and webhook mechanism, so admins and agents can react to changes instead of polling for them

Join the pod's rotations and Hub on-call, and help other builders take on backend work through reviews, guidelines and contract tests

Experience building and running backend systems in production that other teams or customers depend on

Comfort taking a problem nobody owns yet and turning it into a design, a plan and shipped code

Product judgment for technical users: you talk to the people who use your APIs and make trade-offs on their behalf

Clear, short writing that lets a remote team review and decide asynchronously

Care for how a system behaves when parts of it fail, not only for the happy path

Designed and shipped a control plane or similar system that pushes desired state to many targets. Knows outbox or event-log patterns, idempotent apply, versioning, retries and drift reconciliation

Senior backend engineering in Java (API design, relational data models, multi-tenant SaaS services) and hands-on Helm and Kubernetes for self-managed installations

Software architecture and failure analysis: partial failure, blast radius, consistency trade-offs. Has debugged production incidents in a distributed system and is ready for on-call

Has shaped an API or platform as a product, and writes design docs other teams can build on. Gets agreement across teams without formal authority

Builds AI-first from written specifications and checks AI output as carefully as any other code

Kubernetes operators or GitOps tools, for example Argo CD

OIDC, SCIM and permission models, or secret managers and licence systems

Camunda 8 or another workflow or orchestration engine

Enough frontend skill to build a thin UI on top of a backend capability

#7404. Why Rao’s heated up brand building with a request to slow down meals

The Campbell Company sauce brand reimagines the concept of a limited-time offer by keying in on how many meals people have left with loved ones.

Rao’s Homemade today revealed a four-minute short film that reimagines the concept of a limited-time offer by reminding consumers that the most limited time is the time people spend together, per details shared with Marketing Dive. “Limited-Time Only” was directed by Aqsa Altaf and John X. Carey, the duo known as Aqsa + John X. who were behind Dove’s “Real Beauty Sketches” campaign.

“Limited-Time Only” shares the story of three individuals as they are interviewed about how often they have dinner with family. The heart-tugging video culminates with each being presented a jar of Rao’s sauce labeled with an estimate of how many meals they have left with certain family members before being surprised with a dinner with their loved ones.

The short film is part of a larger “Make Time” campaign created with Goodby Silverstein & Partners that spans premium video, social, influencer, public relations, retail media and owned channels.

In a survey of more than 1,000 individuals and in-depth interviews with 200 people, the brand and agency learned that 84% of Americans wish they shared more meals with loved ones, yet only one-third of families actually sit down together. The campaign unites those findings with product truths about the time-intensive way Rao’s sauce is made, from hand-picked tomatoes and hand-peeled onions and garlic that are simmered for hours.

“It's not just about the food; It's about the experience that creating that food enables, and it's an opportunity to really slow down and savor the food and to savor those that you're sitting around the table with, and to make meal time less of a transaction and more of an experience and an opportunity to connect with others,” said Chris Tutor, vice president of advertising and design at Rao’s parent The Campbell Company.

That experience was captured in a series of ads that feature groups of people enjoying a meal and include taglines like “the best part of the day is sharing it” and “keep long stories long.” The spots are set to live orchestral music and end with a scarpetta, the Italian tradition of using bread to mop up the last of the sauce. The creation of those spots led the team to investigate the larger narrative around meals with loved ones that informed “Limited-Time Only.”

“The long-form [video] allowed us to tell more of that story from the impetus for it to the aha moment to then the celebratory moment, and it really allowed us to blow out that visceral moment around the table into the full story,” Tutor said.

While “Limited-Time Only” does not have a traditional ad length, the creative is in line with a trend around longer-form content that marketers have adopted to tell more engaging stories to consumers. Depending on the platform, the brand will either run the long-form asset or teasers that point to the full video.

“There's been a lot of media attention around this idea, and so we’re leveraging media partners to help tell the story,” Tutor said. “Setting up the context before watching the film will also, we think, drive a lot of eyeballs to it. We're trying to do the math and science of making sure the right piece of content shows up on the right channel at the right time.”

The Campbell Company acquired Rao’s parent Sovos Brands for $2.7 billion deal in March 2024. Rao’s is a billion-dollar brand for Campbell’s and continues to grow, with consumption up 11.3% in fiscal year 2026. To fuel this growth, Rao’s increased media spending and will further increase it in fiscal 2027, executives said on a recent earnings call.

While Rao’s has grown household penetration, it still lags behind sister brand Prego. The “Make Time” campaign is key to the brand’s marketing efforts and represents both a brand building story and an awareness play.

“As people become aware of the brand or if they're already aware of the brand, what does the brand stand for beyond just that exceptional food? It's bringing this notion of slow living to every meal, reminding people that life tastes better when it's savored, and establishing what the brand means on an emotional level, versus just a rational level,” Tutor said.
