#7295. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer at Clutch

Clutch builds AI agents that work on behalf of credit unions. Emma, our collections agent, is live at five credit unions and handled 60,000+ member conversations in her first twelve weeks at one of them. Behind her is a growing portfolio: origination, fraud step-up, member (re)engagement and activation agents, all built on the same Agents Platform (TypeScript, NestJS, our own agent SDK, MCP, Temporal, promptfoo).

As a FDE you are one of the two engineers on an agent. You take it from "sold" to "live and performing" at a specific credit union: you sit in the discovery sessions, you build the integrations to their core banking and loan systems, you author and tune the agent's behavior, you run the live tests with their staff, and you stay with it through go-live and the first weeks of production. When something needs a person in the room, you are the one who flies.

This is a senior engineering role, not a solutions or support role. You write production code in our monorepo every week: integrations, modules, and the agents themselves (prompts, tools, evals), together with the other engineers on the pod. The difference from a traditional engineer is that you are on site more often, you make judgment calls in front of the customer, and you own the outcome. What you learn in the field flows straight back into the product: defaults, playbooks, and the platform capabilities the next agent will need.

You build; our AI Operations Managers deliver. The pod (pod lead plus engineers) designs and builds each agent in a high-touch way with the first customers. Once the agent is defined and live, AI Ops coordinates the implementations, configuration and ongoing success at the next credit unions. You hand them something that works and a playbook to run it.

This role requires roughly 20% of travel on average, with one- to two-week stretches around kickoffs and go-lives. Based in the US, able to travel on short notice

You'll join the Agents team, a small, senior group operating as a startup inside Clutch: seven engineers, a dedicated AI product group, and AI sales specialists, working directly with some of the largest credit unions in the country. Every agent is staffed by a pair of engineers, one leaning toward the conversation and LLM side, one toward software and integrations, with a pod lead who owns the product day to day. There is no layer between you and the customer, and no layer between you and the platform team.

The operating style is direct, written, and evidence-driven: BLUF updates, evals over opinions, business outcomes over ticket counts. The team is spread across Brazil, Argentina and the US and works async by default. Being on site is a tool we use at specific moments (kickoff, live testing, go-live), not a permanent posting.

Ship your first production change to a live agent through the team's review process, and be able to explain how a member's call flows through the Agents Platform end to end

Learn one agent deeply: system prompt, tool contracts, orchestration triggers, compliance gates, and the implementation process from kickoff to go-live

Join at least one credit union implementation as the second engineer, including a live testing session with their staff

Map where the current implementation process loses time and where a default or a tool would have removed a round trip

Own the technical side of one credit union implementation end to end: core and LOS integration, agent configuration, testing, go-live, and the first weeks of production

Run the eval and tuning loop for your agent (golden transcripts, rubric judges, regression suite) and be trusted to change its behavior without a second engineer checking your work

Have completed at least two on-site visits and turned what you heard there into product decisions with your pod lead and PM

Contribute at least one reusable capability back to the Agents Platform that the next implementation will not have to rebuild

Have taken one net-new agent from sold to live at its first credit union, with data you can show the partner

Be the engineer credit union staff ask for by name when something in their deployment needs to change

Have written the implementation playbook for your agent well enough that the AI Ops team runs the second and third credit union without you

Be shaping what the next agents look like, because you have seen where the current ones break in the field

7+ years of software engineering in a fast-paced environment, preferably in SaaS and/or Fintech, with senior TypeScript and Node.js backend experience in a large codebase (NestJS, CQRS, schema-validated boundaries, dependency injection). We do not count years of AI experience; the field is too young for that to mean anything. We look at what you have shipped and how you reason about it

Integration and API engineering: secure service-to-service endpoints, third-party APIs, webhooks, and the patience to work with legacy banking systems that were not designed to be integrated

Security and auth judgment: OAuth and machine-to-machine auth, tenant-scoped access, secrets handling, no PII in logs. Our agents touch sensitive member data and operate under consumer-contact regulation

Customer presence: you can sit across from a VP of Collections, hear a request, and say "here is what I recommend and why", including when the answer is no. You are comfortable making calls in the room and owning them afterwards

Code-quality judgment under deadline pressure: you take on debt deliberately, name it, and pay it down, rather than gold-plating or leaving silent debt behind

Genuine appetite for LLM agents: eval discipline for non-deterministic systems, reading a system prompt like code, understanding that some decisions must never reach the model. Curiosity plus shipped work is enough; research depth is not required

Nice to have, explicitly not required: voice and telephony (LiveKit, Twilio), Temporal or event-driven orchestration, Python, core banking or LOS integration experience, credit union or banking domain knowledge

#7293. Staff GTM Systems Engineer at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

The Staff GTM Systems Engineer at LiveKit is the technical owner and architect of the systems that power our go-to-market engine. You'll decide how our GTM stack is designed, what we build versus buy, and where we invest next — and you'll be hands-on building it: Salesforce architecture, integrations, enrichment pipelines, and the agentic workflows that help Sales and Marketing move faster and smarter.

Salesforce is our system of record for revenue, pipeline, and customer data. HubSpot is our marketing layer for campaigns and lead generation, and what it captures needs to flow cleanly and reliably into Salesforce. Around them sits a growing stack of enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and data platform tools. You'll own the architecture that ties it all together: disciplined change management, observable pipelines, data models built for AI consumption, and agentic workflows that act on GTM signals at scale — designed to serve the business for years, not quarters.

This role sits at the intersection of GTM, Data, Finance, and Engineering. You'll work directly with senior leaders to shape the GTM systems roadmap, turn business strategy into technical plans, and guide decisions that span multiple teams. You'll also help grow the engineers around you, setting the technical standards and practices the team builds on.

You understand the difference between owning a system and gatekeeping one. Sales lives in Salesforce. Marketing lives in HubSpot. Your job is to make sure both are governed well enough that others can work in them effectively, and powerful enough that the GTM team can do things they couldn't do before.

You'll thrive as a Staff GTM Systems Engineer if you:

Are known as the go-to person for tough technical problems, and have good judgment about which ones matter most.

Think in systems and long time horizons, and can make confident architectural decisions with incomplete information.

Can clearly explain complex technical concepts and tradeoffs to engineers and executives alike.

Believe systems should be managed with engineering discipline.

Ship fast and reliably — your work holds up in production.

Are a fast learner, frequently picking up new languages and tools.

Find real satisfaction in mentoring other engineers and helping them grow.

Own the architecture of the GTM systems stack: define how Salesforce, HubSpot, enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and the data platform fit together, and write the technical strategy that guides how it evolves.

Lead build-vs-buy and procurement decisions: evaluate vendors, run proofs of concept, assess security and integration fit, and partner with Director of GTM Systems and Analytics, Finance, Legal, and IT on selection and renewals.

Shape the GTM systems roadmap with senior leaders across Sales, Marketing, RevOps, Finance, and Engineering: align on requirements, sequence investments, make clear tradeoffs, and lead cross-functional initiatives to completion.

Own Salesforce as the engineering system of record for revenue: data model, flows and automation, validation rules, field governance, and access control.

Own HubSpot for campaigns and lead generation, along with the HubSpot-Salesforce sync — so leads, contacts, and engagement signals reach the revenue pipeline without drift or silent failures.

Build and maintain integrations across the GTM stack, and partner with GTM Analytics Engineering on reliable data flows to the central data platform.

Design and deploy agentic workflows that consume GTM data, surface actionable signals, and automate manual operations — and set the patterns and guardrails others build on.

Establish observability and change management for critical GTM systems: alerts, dashboards, and runbooks; reviewed code changes, documented config, and rollback paths.

Mentor engineers across experience levels and set the technical standards for the team.

Partner with the Billing Systems Engineer to automate the CPQ-to-Billing handoff.

Extensive engineering experience in GTM or business systems, including owning a GTM stack's architecture end to end — build-vs-buy calls, vendor evaluations, and systems designed for a multi-year horizon.

Deep, hands-on Salesforce expertise — you understand the data model, have built complex flows and automation, and you think about it as an engineering system, not an admin tool. This is the primary bar.

HubSpot experience in a marketing context, including the HubSpot-Salesforce sync and what breaks and why.

Technical depth beyond the UI — you've built integrations with APIs, webhooks, and event streams, and you know when a native tool (a Flow, a Clay table, a HubSpot workflow) is the right answer versus when it's time to write code.

Agentic workflow experience — you've designed and deployed AI agents that act on GTM data in production.

Strong change management practices — you've shipped changes to live systems while protecting active deals and campaigns.

Experience influencing senior stakeholders — translating business strategy into system requirements and roadmaps.

A history of mentoring engineers, from early-career to senior.

Salesforce CPQ: approvals, order forms, discounting rules, and the closed-won-to-billing path.

SQL and data modeling fluency; familiarity with a warehouse-centric stack (BigQuery, Snowflake, dbt, reverse ETL).

Experience with billing platforms (Stripe, Zuora, Maxio) and the handoffs between GTM systems and billing.

Experience managing a GTM tooling budget or vendor portfolio.

Experience as a technical lead on a GTM or business systems team.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

#7291. Week of Nov 2 Evening News Ratings: Election News Lifts Newscasts

Interest in the midterm elections saw ABC World News Tonight with David Muir, NBC Nightly News with Tom Llamas, and CBS Evening News with Tony Dokoupil finish with week-to-week gains in total viewers and the Adults 25-54 demo the week of November 2.

Wednesday’s edition of WNT was the most-watched program on TV, including broadcast and cable, with 9.521 million total viewers. In the demo, ABC News’ evening program had its best showing since January 9, 2025. Meanwhile, Nightly News had its best performance in both measurements in nearly nine months, the week of February 16.

Note: The weekly averages of ABC News and NBC News are based on three days (Monday, Wednesday, and Thursday). Tuesday’s broadcast (11/04/26) of ABC News was coded as a special, and NBC News’ program was preempted. Friday’s (11/06/26) edition of WNT was retitled to WNT-ABC, and NBC Nightly News was retitled to NBC Nitely News. These broadcasts were excluded from the weekly and season averages.

According to Nielsen national live+same-day big data and program ratings, ABC World News Tonight averaged 9.143 million total viewers and 1.325 million Adults 25-54 demo viewers for the week of Nov. 2. The evening newscast was up +8% in total viewers and +15% in the A25-54 demo compared to the previous week. WNT was up +27%* in total viewers and +50% in the demo vs. the same week in 2025 (the week beginning Nov. 3).

NBC Nightly News ranked second in total viewers and the demo, averaging 7.268 million and 1.163 million viewers, respectively. Compared to the previous week, Nightly News grew by +9% in total viewers and +25% in the demo. When comparing its performance to the same week a year ago, Nightly News was up by +9% in total viewers and +25% in the demo.

Finally, CBS Evening News finished the week of Nov. 2 with 4.332 million total viewers and 663,000 demo viewers. CBS News’ newscast saw gains of  +3% in total viewers and +12% compared to the previous week. Its year-ago week numbers showed CBS Evening News up +2% in total viewers and +18% in the demo.

*Note: ABC’s national availability was limited due to a carriage dispute between Disney and YouTube TV.

November 2, 2026 (Nielsen Big Data + Panel):

Source: Nielsen Big Data + Panel, L+SD, Week of 11/2/2026. Season to date: Most Current Data Stream: 2026-2027 Season (9/21 – 11/8/26) and 2025-2026 Season (9/22 – 11/9/26). Average audience based on regularly titled telecasts only.

Mark Mwachiro is a TVNewser contributor who has been writing for the blog since March 2022. Based out of New York, Mark has also contributed to ADWEEK.

#7288. Senior Manager, Business Operations at Clutch

Clutch is at a pivotal moment. We're growing significantly year over year, and we need more connective tissue to keep pace. We're hiring a Senior Manager, Business Operations to connect the commercial side of the house, so the teams that win, onboard, and retain our customers work from the same facts and toward the same goals. This role sits in Finance.

This is a people manager role. You'll lead our Revenue Operations (RevOps) team and act as an operating partner to senior leaders across Sales, Marketing, Customer Success, Implementation, People, and Finance. Together with the RevOps team, you own the shared language of the business (definitions, data, and processes) so commercial teams make decisions from one view of it.

When something important doesn't have an owner yet, it lands with you: a partnership strategy, a new commercial model, or pricing and margin guidance for leadership. This is a fast-moving startup. We're building toward a much larger company but we aren't one yet, and most of this role has no playbook. You'll define the process, build the tool, and hand it to the team. If you want established structure, this likely isn't the role.

You'll report to the Director of Business Operations and work directly with senior leaders across the company as a neutral partner to every function rather than an advocate for one. Your work will be visible, and it will significantly influence how we make commercial decisions.

You'll lead a small, high-ownership RevOps team that runs the systems and data our commercial teams depend on, and creates leverage for them to do their jobs even better. The team doesn't just own systems. They build automation, tooling, and processes, and they're close partners to Sales and Marketing. You'll coach them, set priorities, and open up more strategic scope, while giving them real autonomy.

Learn the business end to end: the revenue funnel, the customer lifecycle, how each commercial team works day to day, and where decisions get stuck

Build trust with your RevOps team: learn the work they own, and set priorities and team rhythms together

Deliver the first version of client-level margin and cost-to-serve, the analysis that grounds our pricing strategy

Develop a first point of view on pricing and packaging for revenue leadership, built on that analysis

Put AI to work in your own workflow from week one, and identify the first set of manual, repetitive processes across the commercial teams worth automating

Stand up a first version of a deal desk, including an approval workflow, pricing and discount guardrails, and standard commercial terms

Partner with Sales and Marketing leaders as they prepare for the monthly business review: pressure-test their plans and point out risks, then solve them with the RevOps team by creating new processes, tooling, or automation

Build on the strength of the RevOps team: prioritize its roadmap against business outcomes and take on more strategic work together

Launch your first AI-enabled workflows or automations that remove manual reporting or reconciliation for the commercial teams, with time saved tracked

Have a team whose output and standards hold, with the processes, tooling, and automation you've built running without you in the room

Have AI-enabled, self-serve tooling that commercial teams use day to day, with measurable time savings

Be the person leadership trusts to pick up the next important thing that doesn't have an owner yet

5-8 years of total experience that combines consulting or banking with time as an operator inside a company

Experience managing people: you've coached ICs, given direct feedback, made tough talent calls, and built team rhythms

Startup experience. You've worked in a fast-moving, ever-changing environment where structure wasn't already there, and you built it. This is not a role for someone whose experience is only at large, established enterprises.

Strong analytical and financial modeling skills, including forecasting. You're very comfortable building models in spreadsheets from scratch, and they hold up to scrutiny. You use AI to move faster and sharpen your work, and you have the foundational modeling knowledge to know when its output is wrong.

Data-first and self-sufficient. You open the dataset before you build the slide, you're always looking for more data to test an idea, and you're comfortable getting into raw data yourself. You use AI to analyze it faster and know enough to check its work.

Commercial acumen: you can read commercial terms, spot the problematic ones, and reason about pricing, discounting, margin, and deal structure

A clear communicator who can walk an executive through a messy problem and leave them with a decision, and who is just as effective working alongside an AE, a CSM, or an engineer

Comfort with ambiguity. When something isn't set up yet, your instinct is to build it, not to wait for it. You create structure, manage competing priorities, and pivot when things change.

#7276. Week of Nov 2 Cable News Ratings: Midterm Elections Bring in Viewers

This is TVNewser’s basic cable network ranker and cable news report for the week of November 2, 2026.

The midterm elections were a boon for the three cable networks, as nighttime coverage of the results and aftermath brought in viewers. As the blue wave spread across the nation on Tuesday, Fox News managed to hold on to its place as the most-watched cable news network in total viewers on election night and remained the news channel of choice for the rest of the week during primetime and total day.

However, it faced strong competition from CNN and MS NOW, with the former taking first place in the Adults 25-54 during primetime and total day. For CNN, its midterm election day and primetime performance was the best since Election Day in 2024.

According to Nielsen Big Data + Panel, for the week of Nov. 2, Fox News averaged 1.862 million total viewers and 228,000 A25-54 viewers during primetime. The network had gains of +14% in total viewers and +48% in the demo compared with the prior week (the week beginning Oct. 26).

During total day viewing, Fox News averaged 1.124 million total viewers and 124,000 A25-54 viewers. Based on the previous week’s average, the network was up by 3% in total viewers and 23% in the demo.

Fox News remained second in total primetime viewership and was third in demo viewership among all basic cable networks. During total day, it remained first in total viewers and dropped from second to fourth place in the total day demo.

MS NOW averaged 1.532 total viewers and 226,000 viewers in the A25-54 demo for the week that just concluded. The network was up 54% in total viewers and 95% in the demo compared with its prior-week primetime average.

During total day, MS NOW averaged 927,000 total viewers and 126,000 demo viewers. It rose by 49% in total viewers and 80% in the demo compared with the network’s average the prior week.

Among all basic cable networks, MS NOW continued to be in third in total primetime viewers and moved from sixth to fourth in the demo. It ranked third in total viewers and the total day demo.

CNN averaged 1.339 million total primetime viewers and 373,000 demo viewers. It registered triple-digit growth of 117% in total viewers and 236% in the demo compared to the previous week.

During total day, the network had 749,000 total viewers and 168,000 demo viewers. For CNN, total viewers were up 77 %, and demo viewers were up 163 % during this daypart.

Among all cable networks, CNN landed in fourth place in total viewers and was the No. 2 network in the demo during primetime. It remained at No. 4 in total day among total viewers and finished in second place in the demo.

#7269. 5 Questions For…CNBC’s Carl Quintanilla on Nvidia CEO’s Shocking Departure

Jensen Huang’s three-decade tenure as the leader of Nvidia came to an abrupt end on Thursday morning amid reports of multiple extramarital affairs, including with a woman alleged to be a Chinese spy. His departure rocks the AI industry at a time of unprecedented growth, with Nvidia as the premier chip supplier.

Huang, who co-founded Nvidia, resigned as president, CEO, and board member effective immediately. According to the company, Colette Kress, the tech firm’s chief financial officer, will fill in as interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor.

The rapid turn of events at Nvidia took place during the morning hours on CNBC, a key time period for the network when viewers are tuning in for the latest on the stock market. Squawk on the Street was on the air when the news broke, and the show’s co-host, Carl Quintanilla, spoke to TVNewser for our latest installment of the 5 Questions For… series about how the news unfolded and broader implications of Huang’s resignation. (All times Eastern)

TVNewser: What happened when the news broke that Jensen Huang had resigned?

Quintanilla: Our morning show Squawk Box was on the air — interviewing a guest about crypto — when the news release hit the wires. Our anchors interrupted and got right to the details. It happened about ten minutes before the top of the hour, so we quickly re-wrote our 9 a.m. lede copy.

The vibe on set, I’d say, was focused and urgent — but it wasn’t newsroom “chaos”, with producers screaming or anything like that. We’d been bracing for news. This thing had been building up to a crescendo all week.

TVN: Why should regular viewers care about what’s going on with Nvidia?

Quintanilla: Well, if they own the stock, they’ve lost nearly $2 trillion. So there’s that. But even if they don’t, it’s a company that designs the backbone of artificial intelligence. It funds and supports dozens of others that are building out AI — which is responsible for nearly half of America’s economic growth.

It potentially could reveal a multi-billion smuggling ring of our most precious technology to the Chinese, and worse case: it could show that the government knew about it and looked the other way. It has the potential to be the biggest corporate scandal in decades.

TVN: Does Nvidia’s performance affect the global economy?

Quintanilla: Not right away. Most of its business for 2027 is already in the books. And even if they lose customers, there are rivals ready to take up the slack. Companies like AMD, Broadcom and Google have already made inroads with their own chip designs. The overall stock market has actually been pretty stable here, and doesn’t seem to be pricing in any kind of economic collapse. The bigger implications may involve the Trump Administration’s oversight, or lack thereof.

TVN: What is the biggest challenge in covering a fast-developing story like this?

Quintanilla: Probably keeping an open mind about what’s still to come. There’s a huge contingent that still wants to give Nvidia every benefit of the doubt, and I get it. The company — and Jensen Huang — were revered. They made investors rich and have created some monumental technology. But as we get reminded every so often, business executives are fallible. Governments and politicians are fallible. We need to be prepared for all kinds of outcomes as the layers of this onion get peeled back.

TVN: What is the most riveting part of this story for you?

Quintanilla: Aw, man. I suppose we’re all just trying to get into Jensen Huang’s head at the moment. How does a guy squander his level of global influence, wealth, and stardom? And for what? For the affair? Was it recklessness? A false sense of invincibility? Or were there other motivations we don’t know about yet? Wall Street analysts have been calling him “Icarus,” which seems appropriate. I just don’t think we’ve ever seen an Icarus fall from this high an altitude.

Mark Mwachiro is a TVNewser contributor who has been writing for the blog since March 2022. Based out of New York, Mark has also contributed to ADWEEK.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

#7262. Changing Lives Productions LLC: Assistant Editor

Changing Lives Productions is the in-house video production company of Dhar Mann Studios, the #1 creator of scripted positive content online. With over 163 million followers and 82+ billion views across YouTube, Facebook, Instagram, and TikTok, our videos receive more than 1 billion views each month.

We produce several hours of original content weekly with messages of compassion, kindness, and altruism that resonate with viewers of all ages. Our stories tackle real-life challenges and deliver powerful life lessons that resonate with global audiences.

Founded by Dhar Mann, named the “Moral Philosopher of YouTube” by The New York Times, a Time Top 100 Creator, and ranked #2 on Forbes’ 2025 Top Creators list—our founding principle is simple but ambitious: We’re not just telling stories. We’re changing lives.

We’re looking for an Assistant Editor who keeps our post-production pipeline organized and moving. You’ll collate and prep project files for our VFX and color teams, and manage the digital assets behind every episode we produce.

This is a hands-on support role. You’ll make sure editors, VFX artists, and colorists get clean, complete, correctly structured files on time, and that every piece of footage, project file, and finished master is ingested, organized, backed up, and archived.

We deliver several hours of original content every week. Missing media, broken links, or poorly prepped turnovers cost our VFX and color teams hours they don’t have.

A well-run library, with clear naming, accurate metadata, and reliable archives, means faster turnarounds, easy re-versioning, and no lost work. You’ll be the person who makes handoffs between editorial, VFX, and color seamless.

HOW YOU’LL SPEND YOUR TIME:Assistant editorial: file collation and handoffs

The Assistant Editor reports to the Jr. Post Production Manager.

You’ll work remotely from home or a personal office and log hours in Dhar Mann Studios’ time-tracking software.

The typical schedule is 40 hours per week. Specific hours and shift overlap with the US team will be discussed during interviews

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

Remember every detail and next step, without a meeting bot.

#7258. Director of Technology Partnerships at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re looking for a Director of Technology Partnerships to lead the teams responsible for growing Supabase through technology integrations and our marketplace ecosystem.

You’ll bring together two complementary motions: partnerships that embed Supabase into the products developers already use, and a marketplace that helps developers discover and connect complementary technologies to their Supabase projects. Your job is to turn both into durable sources of revenue, adoption, and customer value.

Reporting to the Head of Partnerships, you’ll lead and develop the Integrations and Marketplace teams, set their commercial strategy, and work hands-on with our most important partners. A central part of the role is working closely with AI labs to build strategic relationships, develop integrations, and bring joint opportunities to market. You’ll partner closely with Product, Engineering, Sales, Marketing, and Finance to take integrations from idea to launch—and grow their impact long after launch.

This role is based in the San Francisco Bay Area, where you’ll build relationships with leading AI, developer tools, and infrastructure companies. Supabase is remote-first, and you’ll work across a globally distributed team.

Lead, coach, and develop the Integrations and Marketplace partnerships teams, including team leads and partner managers.

Set a clear strategy for where Supabase invests across strategic technology partnerships, marketplace categories, and emerging ecosystem opportunities.

Define team objectives, partner coverage, investment priorities, and operating cadence. Make thoughtful tradeoffs between high-touch strategic partnerships and scalable programs.

Build a culture of ownership, commercial discipline, and strong cross-functional execution.

Drive revenue from technology integrations through partner-driven customer acquisition, product consumption, expansion, retention, co-sell opportunities, and commercial arrangements.

Own the commercial performance of the technology partnerships and marketplace portfolio, including forecasting and reporting on progress, risks, and return on investment.

Build and deepen relationships with AI labs, working closely with their product, engineering, business development, and executive teams to identify and execute high-impact partnership opportunities.

Develop executive relationships with strategic partners across developer tools, cloud, and adjacent infrastructure.

Lead or support negotiations for complex partnerships, including integration, referral, revenue-sharing, and joint go-to-market agreements.

Help partner managers build joint business plans that connect partner priorities to measurable outcomes for Supabase and its customers.

Lead and develop the Marketplace team, setting its commercial and partner strategy and holding the team accountable for outcomes.

Guide the team in identifying the capabilities developers need, recruiting the right partners, and helping those partners launch and grow.

Set priorities and allocate resources across strategic, high-touch partners and scalable paths for emerging and self-serve partners.

Ensure the team works effectively with Product and Engineering on integration quality, partner onboarding, discovery, activation, and the developer experience across product surfaces.

Enable the team to work with Marketing and Sales to launch integrations, enable teams, and drive ongoing adoption—not just announcements.

Establish clear measures for partner-sourced and influenced pipeline, integration adoption, revenue, consumption, retention, and marketplace performance.

Work with Finance, Data, and Partner Operations to improve attribution, reporting, forecasting, and partner revenue processes.

Represent the voice of partners and customers in product and go-to-market planning, while aligning stakeholders around shared priorities.

Identify risks early, remove blockers, and communicate decisions and outcomes clearly to leadership.

Have significant experience in technology partnerships, business development, or ecosystem leadership at a developer tools, cloud, infrastructure, or SaaS company.

Have led and developed partner-facing teams, including managers or senior individual contributors, and can balance strategic direction with hands-on execution.

Have a track record of building partnerships that produced measurable revenue, product adoption, or customer growth.

Have negotiated complex commercial agreements and understand partnership economics, including revenue share, referrals, and consumption-based models.

Have worked closely with Product and Engineering to ship and grow technical integrations. You can engage credibly with technical teams, including those at AI labs, even if you aren’t the person writing the code.

Know how to build relationships with executives while earning the trust of product, engineering, and go-to-market teams.

Use data to prioritize opportunities, assess partner performance, and make decisions when attribution is imperfect.

Are comfortable building in an evolving environment, communicating in writing, and working asynchronously across time zones.

Built or scaled a developer marketplace, integration ecosystem, or partner platform.

Worked with AI LLMs, AI app builders, databases, open-source products, or developer infrastructure.

A background in software development, with an understanding of how developers build and integrate products.

Experience growing both a small number of strategic partnerships and a broader, scalable partner program.

A strong network in the San Francisco Bay Area technology ecosystem.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7254. Technical Account Manager (APJ) at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re seeking a Technical Account Manager (TAM) to be the primary technical partner for our Team & customers after the sale, helping them adopt, operate, and get long-term value from Supabase.

At Supabase, TAMs are the technical owners of their assigned accounts, working closely with our most valuable customers to:

Understand each customer’s architecture, use cases, constraints, and goals.

Validate deployments and guide customers through onboarding and early success.

Proactively surface risks and optimization opportunities before they become incidents.

Coordinate internal teams on complex escalations and drive root cause analysis.

Align Supabase capabilities with the customer’s roadmap.

Successful TAMs are proactive, technically credible, and customer-first. TAM is a relationship, not a response channel: you’ll act as an extension of your customers’ teams and advocate on their behalf within Supabase.

You’ll be joining our Success team, where you will be expected to lead and innovate around the following areas of responsibility:

Act as the technical owner for your assigned accounts, documenting environments, mapping dependencies, and tracking technical risks.

Validate architecture and deployment plans after the sale, guide initial configuration and best practices, and make sure customers understand platform capabilities and limitations.

Help customers reach early success milestones to shorten time to value and reduce early friction.

Regularly review customer environments and usage patterns to identify scalability, performance, reliability, security, and compliance risks and opportunities.

Plan upgrades and lifecycle events, and run architecture reviews for upcoming initiatives, including capacity and growth planning, feature readiness, and early identification of technical blockers.

Coordinate across Support and Engineering on complex or high-impact issues. TAMs are not frontline support, but provide the context, prioritization, and follow-through that drive resolution, root cause analysis, and preventative action.

Serve as the voice of the customer to Engineering and Product, sharing specific, reproducible feedback on recurring pain points and taking part in beta and early access programs.

Partner with Sales and Customer Success on account health and retention, with technical input on renewals, expansions, and evaluations, and early flags on risks to satisfaction.

6+ years of relevant work experience in engineering, SRE, technical consulting, or similar, including operating production systems under real constraints.

Strong technical depth across database internals, scalability, security posture, and infrastructure tradeoffs. You understand a customer’s architecture as well as they do, without escalating every question.

A proactive mindset: you spot risks and optimization opportunities before the customer does, and you treat your accounts like a personal portfolio.

Sound escalation judgment: you know when to escalate, when to resolve internally, and how to communicate clearly under pressure without creating noise.

Very strong communication skills. TAMs explain complex issues to non-technical executives and root causes to senior engineers, often in the same day.

Awareness of how TAM performance shows up in retention and NRR, including surfacing expansion signals and renewal risks early.

Experience working in async or globally distributed teams.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.
